  - [Response templates](response-templates-templated-snippets.md) -- Resolve cases or support issues faster and more efficiently with response templates. Response templates (formerly known as templated snippets) are reusable messages that can be copied to case or task forms to provide quick and consistent messages to users, or to display standard chat response messages to requesters in Agent Chat.
    - [Activate](activate-templated-snippets.md) -- You can activate response templates (formerly known as templated snippets) [com.sn_templated_snip] if you have the admin role. This plugin includes demo data and activates related plugins if they are not already active.
      - [Components installed with response templates](components-installed-with-response-templates.md) -- Several types of components are installed with activation of the Templated Responses (com.sn_templated_snip) plugin, including tables and user roles.
    - [Create a response template](create-modify-templated-snippet.md) -- Create or modify a response template (formerly known as a templated snippet) to define a reusable message that can be copied to case or task forms or used in Agent Chat sessions to provide quick and consistent messages to users.
  - [Policy as Code Engine (PaCE)](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-managing-policies.md) -- You can administrate the full life cycle of policies using PaCE in applications by searching, reviewing, and updating existing policies, and creating policies as required. Policies in the applications are powered by PaCE.
    - [Understanding PaCE](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-understanding.md) -- PaCE enables you to manage, administer, and audit policies from a centralized location.
      - [Key user personas and roles](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-roles.md) -- This section describes different user personas and roles in PaCE. These personas are defined with the application where PaCE is being used.
      - [PaCE life cycle](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-lifecycle-management.md) -- This checklist lists the tasks involved in administering PaCE policies.
    - [Install PaCE](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-installation.md) -- Install PaCE.
    - [Administer PaCE policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-administer-policies.md) -- Use PaCE to manage the life cycle of a policy and create, update, review, and execute policies.
      - [Manage PaCE policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-admin-manage-policies.md) -- You can define and manage your PaCE policies in a single management console.
        - [View PaCE policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-view-existing-policies.md) -- You can view a list of all PaCE policies created by you and other policy administrators. The PaCE management console is your gateway to viewing and updating policies, and managing policy versions.
        - [Create PaCE policy](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-policy.md) -- Create a PaCE policy to determine if changes to a software service or application comply with a set of pre-defined rules.
        - [Activate or deactivate PaCE policy](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-deactivate-policy.md) -- You can activate or deactivate any PaCE policy. Only activated policies can validate object (tables and document IDs) changes. Deactivated policies are not executed, even if the policy is mapped. Deactivating a policy enables you to place the policy into a maintenance-like mode.
        - [Duplicate PaCE policy](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-duplicate.md) -- Create a duplicate of an existing policy.
        - [Edit PaCE policy](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-update-policy.md) -- You can update any existing PaCE policies, whether they are activated or deactivated.
        - [Delete](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-delete-policy.md) -- You can delete a PaCE policy at any time. All related versions for that policy are also deleted. However, for auditing reasons you cannot delete policies that were or are currently in use.
        - [Map PaCE policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-mapping-inputs.md) -- For a policy to be invoked correctly, it must be mapped to an object (table or document ID). The defined mapping settings are automatically verified when the policy is invoked.
          - [Define mapping inputs](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-policy-mapping.md) -- You can map a policy to any number of objects. When you map a policy, validate the inputs, and execute the policy, a compliant, non-compliant, or compliant-exception decision is returned.
          - [Edit mapping inputs](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-edit-mapping-inputs.md) -- You can edit the mapping settings that map a policy to an object.
        - [PaCE mapping](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-mapping.md) -- Static Mapping enables you to map a set of policies to a record or object such as an incident or deployable. Dynamic Mapping enables you to map a set of policies on a dynamic set of records or objects such as a set of incidents or a set of deployables.
          - [Map policies using Static Mapping](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-validate-static.md) -- Use Static Mapping to map multiple PaCE policies against a record such as a change request.
          - [Map policies using Dynamic Mapping](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-validate-dynamic.md) -- Use Dynamic Mapping to map multiple PaCE policies against specified conditions.
            - [Request exception](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-request-exception-dynamic-mapping.md) -- Request an exception on policies that are dynamically mapped to conditions.
        - [Create PaCE policy template](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-policy-template.md) -- Create PaCE templates for the PaCE policy forms that you use frequently.
      - [Manage PaCE policy versions](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-versions.md) -- PaCE policy versions contain the actual policy script, as well as API Variable and Config Parameter definitions used for validating policies. Each policy can have many versions, each of which can be easily managed.
        - [PaCE version states](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-version-states.md) -- A PaCE policy version can be in one of four states: Draft, Current, Archived, and Now-Content. The policy version state also defines the version numbering.
        - [Create PaCE policy versions](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-creating-policy-versions.md) -- You can create new versions for any of your existing policies.
          - [Create PaCE policy version](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-policy-version.md) -- Create a version from scratch at any time for any of your existing PaCE policies.
          - [Create PaCE policy version in low-code](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-policy-version-low-code.md) -- Create a policy version using low-code for any of your existing PaCE policies.
            - [Policy logic fields](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-logic-fields.md) -- Policy logic is a set of conditions that is used for determining whether a policy is compliant or non-compliant. You can use the condition builder to specify conditions for the policy.
          - [Create copy of an existing PaCE policy version](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-copy-version.md) -- You can create a copy of an existing policy version. All the settings and mappings from the existing version are copied over to the new one.
          - [Duplicate PaCE policy versions](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-version-duplicate_2.md) -- You can create a duplicate policy from a policy version or multiple versions from two or more policy versions. If you want to use a locked policy, you must first create a duplicate version and then modify the policy version as required.
          - [Edit PaCE policy script](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-edit-policy-script.md) -- Edit the PaCE policy script to apply any changes to the policy logic.
        - [Publish](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-publish-policy-version.md) -- You can publish a policy version at any time. When the policy is published, a compliant, non-compliant, or compliant-exception decision is made for objects (tables or document IDs) mapped to the policy.
        - [Edit draft policy version](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-edit-draft-version.md) -- You can edit a Draft PaCE policy version at any time. The Draft version can be published to become the Current version. You can also create multiple different Draft versions from different Archived versions.
        - [Edit archived PaCE policy version](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-edit-current-archive-version.md) -- To edit a Current (published) or Archived PaCE policy version, you must create a copy of that version, which can then be updated as required.
        - [Compare PaCE policy versions](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-compare-versions.md) -- You can compare two versions of a PaCE policy, whatever their state. Any differences between the two selected versions are highlighted for quick analysis.
        - [Delete PaCE policy version](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-delete-policy-version.md) -- You can delete a policy version at any time. However, only versions that have never been published can be deleted. Published versions are stored for auditing purposes.
      - [Passing parameters to PaCE policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-passing-parameters.md) -- Parameters can be passed to a PaCE policy to validate updates to an object (tables and document IDs). These variables apply to authoring in both low-code or JavaScript. Policy versions include three types of parameter inputs: API Variables, Config Parameters, and Record References.
        - [Create new variables for policy](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-variable-policy-version.md) -- Create an API Variable, Config Parameter, or Record Reference for the policy version in the Define Variables section of the Policy Builder.
          - [Data type descriptions](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-data-type-descriptions.md) -- The following is a list of the most common data types for creating a variable.
        - [Create data collector](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-data-collector.md) -- Create a data collector for a policy version in the Define Variables section of the Policy Builder.
        - [Data collector structure](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-data-collector-structure.md) -- This section describes the structure of a PaCE data collector.
        - [Activate and build data collectors](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-activate-data-collector.md) -- Activate and build data collectors to add to a policy.
        - [Test data collectors](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-test-data-collector.md) -- Test a data collector for a policy version in the Test Playground.
        - [Add data collectors](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-add-data-collectors.md) -- After testing the activated data collectors, add them to a policy in the Policy Builder.
        - [Add nested policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-add-nest-policies.md) -- Add existing policies to a policy's conditions to define the order a policy's execution and the conditions is executed.
      - [Use Test Playground](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-use-test-playground.md) -- The Test Playground enables you to test updates to a PaCE policy at any time, without the need to test or evaluate them in a Production environment.
        - [Test updates](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-test-policy-version.md) -- Use the Test Playground to test updates to a PaCE policy script. You can evaluate changes to the policy script in real time, enabling you to determine if the changes that are applied render the policy compliant or non-compliant.
        - [Review output decision](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-review-output-decision.md) -- After evaluating any changes made to the PaCE policy script, you can review the execution output and compliance decision reached. You can review this output immediately after evaluating any policy script changes.
        - [Review execution log](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-review-execution-log.md) -- After evaluating any changes made to the PaCE policy script, you can review the execution log generated.
      - [Execute policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-execute-policies.md) -- Execute a policy to determine if the policy is compliant or non-compliant. Executions can be run as part of your policy evaluation process, or in real time when mapped to an object (table or document ID).I presume we should also mention invoking with the API? Do we want to tell the user how to do that exactly?
        - [Review](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-review-executed-policies.md) -- You can review the execution activity of any policy at any time. Recorded execution activity includes the time taken to execute, decisions of compliance, execution types, and the outcome of the execution.
      - [Manage categories and tags](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-manage-categories.md) -- Use categories and tags to manage and group your PaCE policies more efficiently. As the policy administrator, you can create and edit any number of categories and tags.
        - [Manage PaCE categories](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-categories.md) -- Use the Categories option to group your PaCE policies more efficiently. As the policy administrator, you can create and edit any number of categories.
          - [Create](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-create-category.md) -- Create PaCE policy categories to assign to a policy when creating or updating a policy. Categories enable you to categorize and manage policies more efficiently.
          - [Edit or update](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-edit-category.md) -- Update categories for a PaCE policy as required. You can also update categories for Active policies.
          - [View](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-view-categories.md) -- You can view the PaCE policies that have been assigned to a specific category. All the categories created, together with their associated policies, can be viewed in the All Categories table.
        - [Manage PaCE tags](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-tags.md) -- Use tags to manage your PaCE policies more efficiently. As the policy administrator, you can create and edit any number of tags.
          - [Add](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-add-tags-policies.md) -- Add or create policy tags on-the-fly for any PaCE policy, including currently Active policies. Tags enable you to manage policies more efficiently.
          - [Edit or remove](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-edit-tags.md) -- Edit and remove tags from a PaCE policy as required. When editing a tag, you can also define who can view the tag.
          - [View](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-view-tags.md) -- You can view the PaCE policies that have been assigned to a specific tag. All tags together with their associated policies can be viewed in the All Tags table.
    - [Write and test policies](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-write-test-policies.md) -- This section provides guidelines on how to write and test PaCE policies.
      - [Structure of a PaCE policy script](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-policy-structure.md) -- This section describes the structure of a PaCE policy script.
      - [Policy invocation API](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-return-decisions.md) -- When a PaCE policy is invoked, the calling service passes the relevant object (tables and document IDs) that is being validated by providing the documentRecord object. A calling service is a service or product that uses the PaCE API. PaCE determines which policies must be executed by using the mapping table and the documentRecord.
        - [Sample PaCE API](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-inv-api.md) -- The following is a sample invocation request when the PaCE API is executed.
      - [PaCE API invocation parameters](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-invocation-params.md) -- This table describes the properties that can be configured when the PaCE API is invoked. These properties can be set whenever the API is invoked or when you test a policy in the Test Playground.
    - [Policy exceptions](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-exception-about.md) -- By integrating Policy as Code Engine with Integrated Risk Management and Policy and Compliance Management workspace, the Compliance administrator can manage an organization's overall compliance posture and provide a business and regulatory context for codified policies.
      - [Enable](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-exception-enable.md) -- Enable exceptions for a PaCE policy and associate it with a control objective in GRC.
      - [Disable](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-exception-disable.md) -- You can disable exceptions for a policy for which exceptions have been granted. When this option is selected, you can no longer request exceptions and control objectives can no longer be associated with the policy.
      - [Request](policy-as-code-engine-pace/policy-createmap-exception.md) -- After a policy has been enabled and configured for exceptions, you can map a policy to a deployable and request an exception.
      - [Withdraw](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-withdraw-exception.md) -- If an exception is no longer required, you can withdraw a granted exception request for a policy.
    - [Integrate PaCE with other applications](policy-as-code-engine-pace/pace-integrate-other-apps.md) -- Extend the capabilities of Policy as Code Engine (PaCE) by creating policies with other applications.
- [Password Reset and Password Change reports and logs](password-reset/c_MonitorPasswordResetActivity.md) -- The Password Reset application provides several tools for monitoring and troubleshooting password reset activities.
- [Password Reset and Password Change reports and logs](password-reset/c_MonitorPasswordResetActivity.md) -- The Password Reset application provides several tools for monitoring and troubleshooting password reset activities.
- [Unblock a Password Reset user](password-reset/t_UnblockAUser.md) -- If a user is manually banned or is flagged as exceeding max attempts, the user can be blocked (not allowed to use the Password Reset application). You can unblock a blocked user.
- [Password Reset and Password Change reports and logs](password-reset/c_MonitorPasswordResetActivity.md) -- The Password Reset application provides several tools for monitoring and troubleshooting password reset activities.
- [Service instances (Application services)](configuration-management-database-cmdb/application-services.md) -- Understand service instances (application services), learn about different application service types and how multiple ServiceNow business units and products use them.
- [View the change history of application services in classic Service Mapping](configuration-management-database-cmdb/t_ViewCIChanges.md) -- You can view the changes made to an application service as a whole and to the individual configuration items (CIs) comprising the service. Change history is useful for maintenance, planning, or troubleshooting procedures.
- [Compare two versions of an application service in classic Service Mapping](configuration-management-database-cmdb/t_CompareBS.md) -- You can see a summary of application service changes at a glance by comparing two versions of an application service. This feature is useful for checking the application service status before and after a certain change or problem.
- [Create or modify map indicators](dependency-views/t_CreateModifyNGBSMMapIndicators.md) -- Dependency Views maps and application service maps, use icons to display additional information for a CI by displaying its related records such as alerts, outages, incidents and problems. These icons are called map indicator.
